A first draft, and a revise that runs the design or the experiment again, are written as before.
- **Evidence gate** — `engine.evidence_gate` (default on) inserts an `evidence_gate` node between `cross_check` and `write` that weighs the assembled evidence — source count, the cross-check supporting/conflicting balance, whether the run produced real measurements — against the research question **before** any paper is written, and returns a verdict: **sufficient** (write), **broaden** (re-enter the literature loop once more, bounded by `engine.evidence_gate_max_broaden`, default 1; with retrieval off or in `--analyze` mode there is no literature step to re-enter, so it writes instead, the verdict kept for the writer and the evidence level), or **insufficient** (write anyway, but the assessment is handed to the writer so the paper frames its thin evidence honestly rather than over-claiming). Two cases are decided from the counts without a model call, because across 68 recorded gate calls the model answered them the same way every time: a simulation quest with no source with text is **broaden**, and one with at least 15 sources and a finding a source supports is **sufficient**. Before either, a simulation whose experiment ran and produced no results goes back to design once while an iteration is left, and after that is ruled **insufficient** with the reason, so the writer writes an honest failure (the count rule used to pass such a quest on its literature alone); the analysis is told the same. Everything else — and every quest that is not a simulation, runs with `--analyze`, or has retrieval off (`knowledge.enabled: false`, where a broaden could not find a source) — is judged by the model, and the assessment records which decided (`decided_by`). A parse error or provider failure still **routes straight to write** — the flag off does too — but is recorded as `status: "unknown"`, never as a fabricated `"sufficient"`: the evidence status (`core/evidence.py`) reads that field and keeps `publication_ready` out of reach until a real verdict replaces it. Under `rigor_profile: research` an unknown status additionally **pauses the quest** (kind `evidence_gate_unknown`) once: fix the underlying issue and resume, and the gate is tried again; if it fails again on the same evidence, the quest goes on and the gate is a gap (the same holds for the design methodology audit and the claim check). It exists to stop a confident-looking paper from being authored on evidence that doesn't support the question, not to refuse answerable topics. Set `engine.evidence_gate: false` for a no-op passthrough.
- **Research protocol** — a typed `core.protocol.ResearchProtocol` (research question, `topic_type`, `source_policy`, expected evidence, baseline, success metric, replication, stopping criteria, requires-user-input) consolidates the otherwise-scattered clarify slots into ONE validated contract, derived deterministically from the resolved clarify answers + engine config (no extra LLM call). It is handed to the `evidence_gate` so the sufficiency judgement is **contract-aware** — e.g. a topic whose `source_policy` is `web_current` (markets/current events) is judged insufficient if it has only academic sources, because that policy requires recent web sources; a `survey` (history/overview) topic's `mixed` policy accepts credible web / encyclopedic / museum sources so a humanities corpus isn't judged insufficient merely for lacking peer-reviewed papers. Recorded in `state['research_protocol']`. A declarative `core.protocol.ROUTE_MATRIX` is the single source of truth mapping each `topic_type` to the graph path it expects (`simulation` vs `no_simulation`) and the `source_policy` it implies; it is consulted at the design fork so `topic_type` is a first-class routing input. The resolved `no_simulation` flag stays authoritative (config/clarify wins the actual edge), but when the topic type's expected path disagrees with the resolved route the engine logs a `[route]` WARNING in run.log — bidirectional (it also catches a future build/engineering topic that resolved to no-simulation), complementing the earlier clarify-time `topic_shape`-vs-SIMULATE warning.
- **One entry per work** — retrieved sources are de-duplicated on every identity they carry, not just the strongest id: DOI / arXiv id / PMID / URL, and also a normalized title (case, dashes, punctuation, HTML entities and full-width forms folded). The same textbook appendix printed in three books under three DOIs, or an arXiv preprint returned once with its DOI and once without, is one source in the literature, the merged search results and the References. Titles shorter than three words or generic ones ("Introduction", "Preface", "Book review", …) never count as an identity, so different books' chapters stay apart.
- **Machine-readable citations** — the numbered References (scholarly sources) are also exported next to the paper as **`paper/references.bib`** (BibTeX) and **`paper/references.csl.json`** (CSL-JSON), and the Further reading web pages the paper lists as **`paper/further_reading.bib`** and **`paper/further_reading.csl.json`** (a page dropped to keep the paper within its page limit is not in them), so a finished quest drops straight into Zotero / Mendeley / a LaTeX or Pandoc workflow instead of leaving citations trapped in the markdown body. Entry types are chosen from the available fields (`@article` when it has a venue/journal, an `eprint`/`archivePrefix=arXiv` entry for a bare preprint, a `howpublished` web entry for a page), cite keys are stable + unique (`firstauthor+year+word`), and BibTeX special characters are escaped. Both files are listed in the quest's file tree in the Web GUI for direct download.
- **On-demand artifact generation** — produce ONE more output format (PDF / slides / poster / talk) for an **already-finished** quest **without re-running the research**. `Engine.emit_artifacts_only()` loads the quest's LangGraph checkpoint read-only and bundles its on-disk `paper.md` + figures (no node re-runs), then only the requested generator fires. Three surfaces: CLI `python launch.py --config <yaml> --resume <id> --emit paper_pdf`; the Web quest page's **Outputs** panel (a ✓/Generate row per format, showing which the quest is configured for and which exist); and VSCode `@fi /generate [<quest_id>] [<format>]`, which runs in the chat (no terminal), so the formats that call the model (slides / poster / talk) reach `vscode.lm.*` through the chat's own bridge, as a `/resume` does. So a markdown-only quest can get a PDF (or slides/poster/talk) later, on demand, for the cost of one render.
- **Source failure report** — every literature and full-text source (OpenAlex, Crossref, arXiv, PMC, Unpaywall, publisher pages, web pages, figure sources, …) records its failures against the quest that made the request: a rate limit, another 4xx/5xx answer, a timeout, a network error, an unparseable body, a headless browser render that crashed or failed, or full-text fetches abandoned at the time budget. A render whose browser driver dies is one failed source and nothing more — it is caught, reported and left behind, never allowed to end the quest. A 404 is an answer ("not in this index"), not a failure. Each failure is a `[source-failure]` line in `.fi/run.log` as it happens, with credentials redacted, and every run ends with one `[source-failures]` summary line (`none` when clean) plus `.fi/source_failures.json`, written on success, pause and failure alike. A search refused with HTTP 429 is asked again twice, a few seconds apart, before it counts (not when the source asks for a wait over ten seconds or says its allowance is used up). After the literature step, run.log counts the sources that have their full text, only the abstract from the search, or only a title, and says why the text is missing: not marked free to read, free with no download link, free but not downloaded, the requests that failed (by source, with their hosts), and an unset `OPENALEX_API_KEY` when OpenAlex was used. FI reads the `.env` in the folder it runs in and then the one in its own folder. The CLI prints `[FI] source failures: …`, the web quest page shows a banner, and the VSCode chat shows the same line. Before this, a quest could lose every arXiv and OpenAlex result and its log said nothing.
- **Skip diagnostics** — every output kind that fails to produce its primary artifact drops a `<kind>_skipped.md` breadcrumb next to where the artifact would have landed (`paper_pdf_skipped.md`, `poster_pdf_skipped.md`, `slides_skipped.md`, `speech_skipped.md`), explaining what was requested, why it couldn't be produced (missing CLI, refused LLM response, render-tool error, …), and how to fix it. The user discovers the failure by opening the quest folder instead of grepping `run.log`. Diagnostics are idempotent: a subsequent successful run of the same kind removes the stale breadcrumb.
- **Quest failure diagnostic** — when a `_node_*` method raises or a pre-graph stage (preflight, endpoint resolution, executor setup) fails, the engine first sorts the failure (`core/crash_kind.py`), and asks nothing of the person but an action on their own machine or account: a **passing problem** (a timeout, a dropped connection, a 429 rate limit or 5xx the provider's own retries did not outlast) in a step of the graph is run again by the engine itself from the step that stopped, after 1 and then 5 minutes, at most twice per entry into a step and only when the step had been working under 10 minutes in this run (a long experiment is not repeated); a **setup problem** (a file named in the quest's settings that is not there, a setting FI refuses here (Docker that is not running, a PDF with no PDF programs, a Python version it cannot set up, a required skill it cannot use), sign-in, a used-up quota or credit, a model the service does not know, an invalid service address, a service on this machine that is not running, VS Code's connection closed, a file another program holds, a missing program or package, a full disk) names the one action, in one sentence; a programming error raised in FI's own code is said plainly to be **FI's problem**, not the person's settings or research, with nothing for them to fix; anything else is an **unexpected error**, also with nothing to debug (continue later). It then writes `<quest_root>/quest_failed.md`, which leads with what happened, what to do and the `--resume` command, and keeps the exception text, failing-node name (from the LangGraph snapshot when available), provider context and log tail under **Details (for FI's maintainers)**; `.fi/failure.json` holds the same sorted record (`kind`, `title`, `say`, `do`, `node`, `retries`, `at`) for the to-do card, the web quest page and VS Code, and the full traceback goes to `.fi/run.log`. The terminal prints the plain lines (only this run's record) instead of a traceback, and says on screen while it waits to run a step again; the to-do card (`.fi/todo.json`), the web quest page's banner (the exception folded under Details) and the VS Code chat say the same. Any re-run clears the stale file **as soon as it starts** — not only at terminal state — so an in-flight resume that's already running past the node that broke isn't shown as "failed"; if the re-run also fails, the exception handler writes a fresh diagnostic in its place. Same discoverability contract as the skip diagnostics: the failure is visible from the quest directory itself, not just `<quest_root>/.fi/launch.log`. A failure that does not arrive as an ordinary Python error gets the same breadcrumb. Not every failure comes back as one: a driver process dying under a tool the quest is driving can surface outside Python's usual error hierarchy, and a failure of that shape used to skip the diagnostic entirely and leave an empty quest folder whose only trace was a buried traceback. The literature fetch already contains that shape for the sources it wraps (above); this covers the same shape reaching the engine from anywhere else. Stopping a quest yourself is not a failure and writes no diagnostic: Ctrl-C, a shutdown signal and a cancelled run stop it immediately and silently.
- **Cite-by-content references** — prior-work block carries author / year / title / venue / DOI, and the engine's References entries carry the same fields. The writer cites only the prior-work block's labels and writes no source list; the prompt forbids fabricating a source or embedding fenced code in the body.

### Cost + observability

- **Engine cost instrumentation** — `core/provider.py:MODEL_PRICING` + `estimate_cost_usd(...)`. Every model call appends `{ts, node, model, usage, cost_usd}` to `<quest_root>/.fi/cost.jsonl` through `core/provider.py:append_cost_row`: the engine's nodes and the output generators (`slides`, `poster`, `speech`, `visual_check`). `cost_usd` is an estimate from the table's rates, and it is `null` for a model the table does not list: a newer model that merely contains a listed name (`gpt-5.6-luna` contains `gpt-5`) is a different model with its own price, so it does not borrow that row, and its token counts are still logged. Char-based token estimate (~4 chars/token) when the transport returns no `usage`; row carries `usage.estimated: true`. Quest finalization writes `<quest_root>/.fi/cost.summary.json` with totals + per-node + per-model breakdowns, and the output pass writes it again so the generators' calls are in it. `total_cost_usd` (and each bucket's `cost_usd`) adds up the calls that carry a price and is `null` only when none do, so a quest that mixes priced and unpriced models has a total that is a lower bound: the summary then carries `total_cost_usd_partial: true`, and `unpriced_requests` (in the totals and in every `by_node` / `by_model` bucket) counts the calls whose `cost_usd` is `null`; the token counts are always complete. The web quest page and compare page read the same roll-up from `/api/quests/<id>/cost` and append "(partial: N calls unpriced)" to the dollar figure when the total is partial; a total that is `null` shows as before. The CLI and the VS Code extension print no cost figure, so nothing there changes.

### Interviews + frontends

- **Unified interactive interview** — CLI `--new`, VSCode `@fi /new`, web `/interview` all drive `core/interview.py` (snapshot in `core/interview_schema.json`). Tier 1 asks topic, *What is the result for?* (research, the default, or a decision writes `rigor_profile: research`; exploring leaves the default profile, writes `result_use: explore` (an exploration is never publication-ready: `core/evidence.py`) and writes three cheaper engine settings — the same answer writes the same settings on every interface, and the review screen lists the reviewers the answer runs before anything starts), provider + model (CLI and web only; VSCode uses its chat model), then, for research or a decision only, *A different model for one reviewer* (research needs one reviewer on another model: the provider's other models, on VSCode the other chat models it offers (a typed name must be one VS Code offers), or *I only have one model*; the quest's own model must be named for research or a decision, and cannot be picked again here; a model is written as `provider.node_models["review_panel.statistician"]` unless the per-node models already put a reviewer on a model other than the quest's own, and *I only have one model* as `engine.one_model_review: true`, written only while no reviewer is on another model; `--update` drops `engine.one_model_review` when it rewrites the settings and a `review_panel.*` per-node model other than the quest's own is set (a flag left in a config edited by hand is harmless: the gap applies only while every reviewer really is on one model); each question's `ask_if` in the schema says when it is asked) — three steps in all (`FIRST_STEPS`: the research question, what the result is for, the model); tier 2 is worked out from the topic and shown for review on **four plain cards** (`REVIEW_CARDS`, the same on every interface: how strictly it is checked and what it costs — the result use, the reviewers, confirm on unseen data, and a call-count estimate; the model and whether it is ready — the provider-readiness sentence; data, sources and pauses; what you get — including the folded *Paper byline (optional)*), each card's rarer settings under one *Advanced* part, values in plain words (`PLAIN_VALUES`: never `paper_md` or `methodologist`) — the paper format, deliverables and study depth (a changed paper format works the study depth and what follows out again, except what was set by hand), the title, research approach, reviewers, retrieval, and the mid-quest pause for your own papers / datasets; tier 3 holds the advanced slots, including the multi-model ensemble and its models, paper style, poster size, the design-revise iteration budget and reasoning effort (`provider.reasoning_effort`). Pinned values land in `engine.clarify_overrides`. Every question declares which surfaces ask it, and the schema-parity test derives its expectations from that declaration, so a question cannot ship unasked on one interface while CI stays green. **What was approved is what runs** (`core/plan_settings.py`): the first start of a quest the interview wrote records, in `.fi/approved_plan.json`, the settings that decide how strictly it is checked (the rigor profile, the reviewers, what each check does when it fails, the cross-check and its verification, the iteration budget, where the experiment runs and in what environment, whether the plan waits for you, whether it explores first and confirms once on data or seeds it never saw). Every later start compares; a setting edited by hand since then stops the quest before anything runs, with each change named as approved and as it is now in `NEXT_STEP.md`. `--update` (web: the quest page's Update; VS Code: `@fi /update`, which runs in the chat, shows why the quest is waiting and asks you to approve the settings in `config.yaml` as they are or opens the file first; only that approve choice skips the questions: `--update` run with no terminal any other way approves nothing) shows the changes and records them, which is how one is approved; putting the setting back undoes it. The model (provider, model, per-step models) is recorded too but never stops a quest: a resumed quest whose model changed takes it, says so in one line (*the model changes from A to B from here on; steps already done were made by A*, or *nothing had run on A yet*, also naming single steps `provider.node_models` keeps on A), records a `model_changed` event in its trace, and the paper is told that more than one model produced it (not when nothing had run on A); under `rigor_profile: research` a change that leaves every reviewer on one model still stops (the reviewer-models check, also after the panel has reviewed once). The multi-model ensemble (`provider.node_ensemble`) decides how many models vote on a check, so a change to it still stops. Resuming with another config (`--config edited.yaml --resume <id>`) updates the quest's own `config.yaml` to match it (each earlier one kept as `config.yaml.before-resume-<time>`), so the next resume does not go back to the old model. A config written by hand was never on a confirm screen, so it is not recorded. Deleting the record does not re-approve anything: the audit trace remembers one was written (with `engine.audit_trace: false` it cannot, and a deleted record is written again); `--update` then says there was no record and approves the settings as they are. A record that is there but cannot be read (not JSON, another schema, its fields missing) stops the quest the same way and is never written again from the config as it is now, and the record's own hash is kept in the audit trace at every approval (the first start and `--update`), so a record edited by hand to agree with a weaker config stops the quest too, as does a trace whose chain no longer checks out; FI's own rewrite of the record (a default of an older FI) records its new hash; `--update` approves again, also before the first run. On `--update`, pressing Enter keeps each answer's current value (it used to take FI's own default, so a changed setting went quietly back).
- **Mid-quest interview re-entry** — `--update <id>` / `@fi /update <id>` / `POST /api/interview/update/{id}` re-open the interview with editable fields. The form pre-fills from the quest's existing YAML (not interview defaults), so unchanged fields round-trip cleanly; `STAGE_INVALIDATION` decides which downstream nodes re-run when an editable field changes. Hand-crafted `provider.node_ensemble` blocks the interview can't classify back to a preset are preserved across update saves. `no_simulation` is hard-locked mid-quest. In VSCode the update runs in the chat, not in a terminal: it resolves a short id or a quest from another folder as `/resume` does, shows why the quest is waiting, and asks whether to approve the settings in `config.yaml` as they are (it then runs `--update` with no questions, its model calls through the chat's own bridge) or to open the file first; for answering the questions one by one it shows the exact `--update` line to run in a terminal yourself, carrying the address of the extension's session-long bridge and the quest's output root, so a non-default output directory resolves to the quest that was actually offered. `--update` run without a terminal any other way (a pipe, a script) asks its questions and so approves nothing.
- **Status GUI** — FastAPI server at `web/static/index.html`: fleet list, SSE log stream, clarify panel, paper preview, panel-review cards. Tools dropdown with ARIA semantics (`aria-haspopup` / `aria-expanded`) + Escape-to-close + first-menuitem auto-focus on open.
- **Web UI quest launching** — `--serve` spawns `python launch.py --config <yaml>` via `web/quest_launcher.py` (capped at `--max-concurrent`). Detail page auto-refreshes every 3 s. Non-loopback bind logs a WARNING.
- **In-process quests over the API** — `POST /api/quests/start` with `{"yaml": "<config>"}` runs the quest inside the server process instead of a subprocess, answering its clarify and human-review pauses through `/api/quests/{id}/clarify` and `/api/quests/{id}/human-review`. When the engine finishes, the server runs the same output pass as the CLI: `paper.pdf`, slides, poster and talk per `output.kinds`, the visual check, and `frontier_insight_summary.json`, so the same config produces the same files either way. The paper's pandoc and LaTeX compile runs in a worker thread, so the server keeps answering other requests meanwhile. A generator that raises is written to the quest's `.fi/run.log` and listed under `errors` in `GET /api/quests/{id}` (`stage` is `paper`, `slides`, `poster` or `speech`, `outputs` when the pass stopped, as it does under `output.require_pdf`, or `quest` when the engine run itself failed); the server keeps running.
- **Web UI "everything" pass** — `/tools/{proposal,critique,digest,portfolio,summarize,analyze,fleet,ingest}`, file browser, zip download, resume button, clarify-resume panel, labels, cost chart, paper iterations, exec edit (gated by `FI_WEB_ALLOW_EXEC_EDIT=1`), trash bin at `/trash`, `/compare?a=&b=`, `/settings`, `/about`. Self-hosted Markdown renderer (`md_lite.js`).
- **Bridge-aware vscode_extension across all serve forms** — `/interview`, `/update/<id>`, and every `/tools/*` form surface `vscode_extension` at the top of the provider picker when a VSCode bridge is reachable. The model dropdown lists **every** chat model VSCode exposes to the extension, tagged with the vendor it came from — Copilot's catalogue, and anything registered by another extension's language-model provider (an Ollama server, a BYOK OpenAI-compatible endpoint). Picking one routes the quest through it. The schema endpoints **TCP-probe the bridge port and IPC-probe the bridge socket** before reporting availability, so a stale or wrong configuration can't lie to the UI. Without a reachable bridge the entry stays hidden — same source of truth as the submit-time 400 guard. Model dropdowns offer "Other (type your own)" with a text-input fallback on every page. The picker's selection is threaded through to the spawned subprocess via a `--<tool>-model` flag for every LLM tool (proposal / critique / digest / portfolio / summarize / analyze), so the picked id reaches `ProviderConfig.model` and the bridge actually routes to the chosen model instead of falling through to the Copilot Chat-picker default.
- **id-first model selection on the VSCode bridge** — `selectChatModels` is queried with `{id: hint}` first, then `{family: hint}`, in both `PersistentBridge` (`--serve` / tools path) and the per-chat-command `Bridge` (`@fi /start` path). The picker stores model **ids** (e.g. `gemini-3-flash-preview`), and Copilot's family is sometimes coarser (`gemini-3-flash`), so filtering by family alone misses every versioned/preview model. Family fallback keeps the legacy `gpt-5` / `claude-opus-4-7` style hints working. When nothing matches, the error dumps the full `id|family` catalog so the next mismatch is self-diagnosing.
- **Per-user IPC PersistentBridge for --serve / --tools** — the FI VSCode extension starts a session-long bridge at activation, listening on a per-user OS-managed address (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/fi-bridge.sock` on POSIX with `/tmp/fi-bridge-{uid}.sock` fallback, `\\.\pipe\fi-bridge-{USERNAME}` on Windows). `--serve` auto-resolves the same path via `core.bridge_path.persistent_bridge_path()` and routes LLM calls through `vscode.lm.*` with no port to wire and no env var to set. Shared-Linux hosts isolate cleanly (each UID gets its own socket); per-chat-command bridges (`@fi /start`, `@fi /digest`, etc.) keep using their existing per-spawn TCP transport. Opt-out via `--no-vscode-bridge`.
- **Bridge stall-timeout recovery** — when Copilot's HTTP/2 stream stalls mid-response, both the TS-side bridge (per-chunk inactivity timer) AND the Python-side `VSCodeBridgeClient.chat` (outer `asyncio.wait_for` against `provider.cli_timeout_s`, default 300 s) surface a `bridge stalled` error. The phrasing is load-bearing: `_TRANSIENT_BRIDGE_MARKERS` in `core/provider.py` matches it so tenacity retries the call. Older `.vsix` builds (no TS-side timer) or a TCP/IPC blip that drops the `lm_error` itself can no longer wedge the engine.
- **CLI streaming + inactivity watchdog** — `claude_cli` is invoked with `--output-format stream-json --include-partial-messages` so Sonnet 4.6's `thinking_delta` events arrive as a steady pulse during extended-thinking spans that otherwise look identical to a hung process (the old `--output-format text` mode buffered the entire response, including 9+ minutes of pre-answer thinking on complex prompts, into one silent stdout flush). The reader resets a soft `cli_inactivity_timeout_s` watchdog (default 180 s) on every stream event, while the hard `cli_timeout_s` ceiling still applies as a last-resort kill; per-node overrides in `provider.node_cli_timeout_s` raise that ceiling for reasoning-heavy nodes (`implement` → 1800 s, `execute_reflect` → 900 s) without bumping the cheap nodes. Setting `provider.cli_timeout_s` yourself makes it a floor rather than a fallback: the built-in per-node defaults are raised to it and the change is logged, so a config asking for 900 s no longer silently gets 600 s on the outline node — while a `node_cli_timeout_s` map you write yourself names only the steps it changes: those are honoured exactly as written, including a deliberately tighter budget that catches a hang on one node faster, and every other step keeps its built-in budget (raised to the floor). `Engine._chat` wires a heartbeat callback that logs progress every ~30 s: `[implement] still waiting on LLM — thinking (2400 thinking-token events), elapsed=4m22s, idle=0s`. The **non-streaming CLIs also heartbeat** — `codex_cli` (`last_message_file`, stdout DEVNULL'd) and `copilot_cli` / `gemini_cli` (plain stdout) have no token-level signal, so the `communicate()` path fires the same callback on a ~10 s timer; the line reads `still waiting on LLM — no events yet, elapsed=Ns`, so a long silent codex/copilot call no longer reads as "pending" on the dashboard.
- **CLI output gate registry** — vendors sometimes deliver upstream-state text (rate-limit messages, give-up placeholders, policy refusals) as plain `text_delta` events instead of structured error envelopes. Without protection, the engine aggregates that text and writes it to disk *as* the artifact — observed in production where `paper.md` became literally `"You've hit your session limit · resets 2:30am (Europe/Brussels)"`. `_collect_via_streaming` and `_run_cli` both run the assembled response through `_check_output_gates`, a registry of named gates: `rate_limit_message`, `placeholder_response`, and `refusal`. Each gate has its own marker list and a 500-char length cap so a real paper-length output that mentions any marker in body text doesn't false-positive. The first gate that fires names itself in the raised `_CliTransientError` so retry logs say *which* gate triggered. Tenacity retries (and may escalate the model via the per-node fallback table — see model-escalation bullet).
- **Jittered retry backoff** — every tenacity retry policy in `core/provider.py` (HTTP direct, VSCode bridge, CLI exec) uses jittered waits (`wait_random_exponential`, or on the HTTP path's server-outage schedule ±20 %) instead of a deterministic `wait_exponential`. A deterministic schedule synchronises N concurrent fleet quests on a shared upstream blip — they all retry at the same wall-clock instant and re-clog the upstream. Random-exponential picks uniformly from `[0, multiplier · 2^attempt]` capped at `max`, spreading retries over a window so the upstream sees a gentler ramp. The HTTP path's longer outage waits (see the retry-classification bullet) vary each wait by ±20 % for the same reason. Eliminates a thundering-herd failure mode that's invisible until you run a busy fleet.
- **Tenacity model escalation on retry (opt-in)** — `provider.node_model_fallbacks` maps node name → fallback model and is **empty by default**: FI never picks a model on your behalf. When you set it, on retry attempt 2+ `_chat_cli` switches that node's CLI invocation to the fallback model (attempt 1 always uses your primary model). Empirical motivation: Sonnet 4.6 + extended-thinking on long code-gen prompts (implement-body, write) reproducibly paralysis-thinks (verified 3× across prompt size and output shape variants, all hit 15-min wall with zero text); in that test, a retry on Opus 4.7 landed the same prompt in ~5 min. To opt in, name a stronger model for the nodes you want, e.g. `implement: <your stronger model>` and `write: <your stronger model>`; the string is passed straight to whichever CLI is active, so it has to be a model that provider accepts (see `docs/PROVIDERS.md`). Standalone CLI matrix (verified on this very prompt): `claude_cli`+`claude-opus-4-7` works, `codex_cli` works, `copilot_cli`+`gpt-5-mini` works, `gemini_cli` aborts via its own loop-detector, `copilot_cli` default model times out.
- **Long stream lines and long answers** — a claude stream-json line holds a whole message, and one carrying a long thinking block runs past 100 KB. The CLI's stdout is read with a 256 MiB line limit (asyncio's default is 64 KiB: past it the reader used to stop as if at the end while the CLI, its pipe full, could not exit, which is what the "wedges" on heavy `claude_cli` prompts were); a line past even that is drained and named in the error, never taken for the end. An answer longer than the output limit arrives as several turns (the CLI stops one at `max_tokens` and asks the model to resume), and its result envelope holds only the last: the turns cut off that way are put back in front of it, so a long script or plan comes back whole.
- **Wedge fail-fast** — a CLI that closes stdout with **no answer** and does not exit is raised as a distinct `_CliWedgeError` and **bailed after the 2nd occurrence** instead of burning the full 4-attempt budget. The error says to resume, and to use a different provider for that node (e.g. `--config <codex_or_openai>.yaml`) if a resume stops the same way, so `quest_failed.md` tells the user how to recover.
- **Tenacity retry visibility** — the `_chat_cli` retry loop wires a `before_sleep` callback that logs the caught `_CliTransientError` text + the next attempt's model to `run.log` as a WARNING. Without this, transient retries were silent (caught exception text dropped) and write-attempt-1 deaths showed up only as a sudden `elapsed=1s` heartbeat with no diagnostic.
- **CLI input-cap guard** — a CLI provider with a hard per-turn input limit (`codex_cli` rejects turns over 1,048,576 chars with `input_too_large`) carries a `max_input_chars` on its `_CliSpec` (codex_cli: 900K, sitting under the limit to leave room for codex's own system prompt + tools schema). When a node's prompt exceeds it — e.g. `analyze`/`write` after an evidence-gate **broaden** pulled in many literature sources — `_run_cli` trims the **middle** of the prompt (keeping the task head + the output-format tail, where FI always puts the load-bearing instructions) and logs a WARNING, so the call goes through instead of hard-failing the quest. Trimming is lossy (some literature/result detail is dropped); the warning points at the leaner-footprint knobs (`knowledge.top_k`, `knowledge.literature_excerpt_chars`, `engine.evidence_gate_max_broaden: 0`). **`copilot_cli` carries the same cap for a different reason** — it passes the prompt as a command-line **arg**, so on Windows the whole command line hits the `cmd.exe` ~8191-char limit (`copilot.BAT` fails a long design/write prompt with `rc=1: The command line is too long`); its `max_input_chars` is 7000, well under that. 7 KB is small for a rich node prompt, so copilot trims heavily on long-context nodes — the real fix is a stdin/temp-file transport, so prefer `codex_cli` / `openai` for long prompts on Windows. The stdin-based CLIs (`claude_cli` / `gemini_cli`) have no arg-length limit.
- **Provider fallback chain + circuit breaker** — `provider.fallback: [codex_cli, gemini_cli]` (default empty) lists providers to try, in order, when the primary terminally fails a chat call (after its own retries). Each provider carries a circuit breaker: after repeated failures — or one auth/quota/credit error — its circuit opens and it's skipped, so subsequent calls jump straight to a live provider instead of re-burning the dead one's retry budget every call. The breaker is **half-open**: after a cooldown (`breaker_cooldown_s`, default 90 s) one probe is let through, and a provider that has recovered rejoins the chain — so a transient blip doesn't permanently drop it for the rest of a multi-hour quest. Fallbacks are built lazily (nothing is resolved and no proxy is spawned until the primary actually fails); `last_model`/`last_usage` track the serving provider so cost accounting stays correct; a `CancelledError` never triggers a fallback. A single provider outage (rate limit, auth failure, proxy crash) no longer forfeits the whole quest.
- **Global LLM-call backpressure** — `FI_MAX_CONCURRENT_LLM_CALLS` (default `0` = unlimited) caps the number of in-flight provider calls across every quest in one process. A `--fleet` of N quests each fanning out a review panel / ensemble can otherwise burst into dozens of simultaneous requests and trip provider rate limits; set e.g. `8` for a large fleet. The slot wraps only one dispatch, so fallbacks re-queue rather than deadlock; a call's own retries run inside that dispatch, and the HTTP path gives the slot back while it waits out a provider outage, so minutes of waiting do not hold slots other calls are queued for. Single quests see no change at the default. **Scope caveat:** the cap is per-OS-process. `--fleet` runs its quests in one process, so the value is a true aggregate ceiling. `--serve` launches each quest as a separate subprocess, so each gets its own cap and the effective ceiling is `FI_MAX_CONCURRENT_LLM_CALLS × --max-concurrent` — size the value with that multiplication in mind under `--serve`.
- **Retry classification** — the provider retry loops distinguish *transient* failures (5xx / 429 / network blips, and CLI `OSError` / transient markers) from *deterministic* ones. A 4xx (bad key, content policy, oversized body) and an hours-long CLI session/usage/credit-limit or auth failure abort immediately with the original message instead of burning the 4-attempt jittered-backoff budget on an error that can't clear in the retry window. On the HTTP transport, a server outage or rate limit (a 5xx other than 501/505/524, including Cloudflare's 520–523 and 525–529, or a 429 that is not a used-up quota) gets six attempts with waits of about 10, 20, 40, 60 and 90 s (±20 %, about 3–4.5 minutes in all). A server's `Retry-After` of at most 120 s is taken as given after a 429 and as a floor after a 5xx; one call waits at most 5 minutes in all. The budget stays at six once any attempt in the call met an outage status, so a read timeout between two 503s does not cut it short. A 429 is a used-up quota when its error type or code names one (`insufficient_quota`, `exceeded_current_quota_error`; a type that also names a rate limit, such as `too_many_requests_error` / `token_quota_exceeded`, does not count) or its message names a monthly, weekly or daily limit (Ollama's `api_error` "reached your monthly usage limit"), and no sane `Retry-After` came with it; such a 429 fails at once and opens the fallback circuit at once, like a 401/402/403. A dropped connection or read timeout on its own, a 501/505/524, and any call while a later `provider.fallback` provider with a closed circuit can take it (a half-open probe included) keep the four short attempts, so a fallback chain moves on instead of waiting out a dead provider; when every later provider is tripped too, the call waits the outage out as it would with no chain. The concurrency slot is given back during each outage wait and released exactly once, so a cancel is never held up waiting for a free slot. A Moonshot 521 once ended a quest after four attempts about a second apart; the longer wait rides out that kind of few-minute outage (see `docs/PROVIDERS.md`). A CLI reporting that its model's server has no capacity (HTTP 503, `UNAVAILABLE`, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`: the server's state, not the account's quota) is transient, and waits 30–90 seconds between attempts instead of at most 20, since capacity takes longer than a dropped connection to come back. A call **killed for running out of wall-clock** is retried with a *longer* budget (1.5× per attempt, capped at 2.25×), because retrying the same work under the same ceiling reproduces the timeout — one node burned three attempts and about 40 minutes that way. Capacity errors and wedges keep the configured budget: neither is a "needed more time" failure.
- **A failed check is recorded, a failed review stops** — a provider blip in the evidence gate, the design audit or the claim check can't forfeit a finished paper: the quest goes on and the check is recorded as `unknown` (never a pass), which keeps `publication_ready` out of reach until it runs again. The review is stricter: a reviewer (or a panelist) that cannot be asked, or answers twice without a verdict, stops the quest (`_pause_for_review_unavailable`), and resuming asks again — no verdict is ever made up for it. A panelist that did not review casts no vote, and an accept no reviewer gave is never auto-accepted (see `docs/rigor.md`). `CancelledError` still propagates cleanly so a real cancel isn't swallowed.
- **Deterministic gate routing** — judgment nodes (`evidence_gate`, `review`, `review_panel.*`, `claim_check`, `cross_check`, `relevance_guard`) run at temperature 0 so their routing decision (sufficient vs broaden, accept vs revise, supported vs not) is reproducible run-to-run instead of flipping and triggering wasted broaden loops or spurious revise cycles. Applies wherever the transport honours temperature (HTTP + vscode_bridge); generative nodes keep the 0.2 default. CLI transports that don't expose temperature are unaffected.
- **Per-node HTTP timeout + prompt-size guard** — the OpenAI-compatible transport takes a per-node read-timeout (`provider.node_http_timeout_s`, base `provider.http_timeout_s` = 120 s), mirroring the CLI's `node_cli_timeout_s`: reasoning-heavy nodes (`implement` → 900 s, `write` → 600 s, every repair that returns a whole script — `execute_reflect`, `implement_seed`, `implement_oracle`, `implement_protocol` — and `plan` / `plan_revise` → 600 s, …) get headroom while cheap nodes keep the tight base that catches a hung server fast. A retry starts the answer over, so a node left at the base that needs longer fails every try: four 120 s tries end the call after 8 minutes with nothing. A `node_http_timeout_s` map in the config names only the steps it changes; every other step keeps its default (an empty map `{}` changes nothing). Raising `http_timeout_s` raises every built-in step budget below it too, so a higher base never leaves a step shorter; a budget you write in the map is kept as written. Every failed call that is tried again leaves one line in the quest's `run.log` — the step, the provider and model, the attempt of how many, the error and the wait before the next try (HTTP, CLI and VS Code connections alike, and the calls the slides, poster, speech, visual check and critique make for a quest; anything that looks like a key, including one in a URL, is removed). The digest, portfolio, proposal and folder summary belong to no one quest, so their retries go only to FI's own log. A transport-agnostic `provider.max_prompt_chars` (default `0` = off) generalizes the CLI middle-trim to every transport — when set, a prompt over the cap is trimmed in the middle of its largest message (head + tail kept) with a loud warning, so a runaway prompt can't blow the context window into a hard 400 / stall.
- **Bridge reconnect-on-drop** — a mid-quest VSCode-bridge socket drop nulls the stale transport so the next chat reconnects, instead of leaving a dead writer that wedges the rest of a long `--serve` / `--tools` session. In-flight calls fail with a transient `BridgeError` (retried), and pending awaiters are unblocked rather than hanging.
- **Fleet isolation + memory-cap safety** — in `--fleet`, a quest whose Engine construction fails is isolated (siblings still complete; the runner exits non-zero) instead of aborting the whole batch, and a `--memory-cap-mb` set below the baseline RSS — which would deadlock the fleet (nothing can run to free memory) — is disabled with a warning rather than hung on. `--profile` is rejected with `--fleet` (viztracer can't meaningfully trace N concurrent quests in one process).
- **Resume completes missing outputs** — a `--resume` regenerates only the outputs actually missing on disk: a kind whose final deliverable already exists (`paper.pdf` / `slides.pdf` / `poster.pdf` / `talk.md`) is carried through untouched, so resume no longer re-invokes the LLM for slides/poster/speech that already rendered or clobbers good decks. **Existence isn't trusted blindly** — a PDF must be non-empty *and* start with the `%PDF` magic, and a text deliverable must be non-empty, so a truncated file left by a kill mid-compile is regenerated rather than shipped. Any configured output still missing after the pass is reported. `--emit` stays an explicit force-regenerate path.
- **Resume self-heals a half-built venv** — `VenvExecutor.setup()` reuses a per-quest `.venv/` only when the interpreter actually exists *and* a cheap `import pip` probe passes (CPython writes `pyvenv.cfg` before copying the interpreter and running ensurepip, so a run killed mid-build leaves a `pyvenv.cfg` with no interpreter). A broken venv is rebuilt from clean on the next `--resume` instead of spawning a missing-python `FileNotFoundError` on every retry.
- **Unknown config keys fail fast** — `Config.from_yaml` audits the top level and the six known sub-sections against their real fields and **raises** on an unrecognized key, listing the offending dotted paths. Pydantic's default is to silently drop unknown keys, so a typo like `provider.node_http_timeoutt_s` would otherwise validate cleanly and quietly revert a reliability knob to its default. Legacy pause flags and free-form dict fields (`node_models`, `node_*_timeout_s`, `clarify_overrides`, …) are still accepted.
- **Windows MAX_PATH venv diagnostics** — a deep `output_dir` on Windows can push the per-quest venv's native-extension paths (PIL/matplotlib `.pyd`) past the 260-char limit, silently killing figure generation. `setup()` warns when the venv path already exceeds ~200 chars, and `execute()` sniffs a DLL-load failure in stderr and logs one actionable hint (enable `LongPathsEnabled` or shorten `output.output_dir`) — so even the otherwise-silent web-figures path surfaces the cause.
- **Stuck-quest dashboard surfaces** — `/api/quests/{id}` returns `node_started_at`, `node_elapsed_s`, `node_idle_s`, and `quest_failed: {present, failing_node, what_broke, kind, title, say, do}` derived from `run.log` timestamps, `<quest_root>/quest_failed.md` and `.fi/failure.json`. The detail page (`web/static/quest.html`) renders a colored chip next to the current-node label (green = active, yellow = idle ≥5 min, red = idle ≥10 min) and an error banner when a `quest_failed.md` is present: what happened and what to do first, the failing node and the one-line exception folded under Details (shown open for a file an older FI wrote). The banner clears the moment a resume **starts** — the engine deletes the stale file at the start of every run (not only at terminal state), so an actively-running resume isn't mislabeled "failed"; a re-run that fails again writes a fresh diagnostic. Because that idle chip is driven by `run.log` recency, the **execute node emits a heartbeat** (`[execute] running experiment.py — still running, Ns elapsed`, every 30 s) while the experiment subprocess runs — otherwise a multi-minute parameter sweep, which the executor just blocks on silently, would show as idle/red the whole time even though it's hard at work.
- **"Needs you" is distinct from "complete"** — a paused quest still writes `frontier_insight_summary.json` on its clean rc=0 pause-exit, so a single `_quest_pending()` helper (shared by the dashboard scan and `/api/quests/{id}`) decides whether the quest is actually *waiting on the user* — via the unified `NEXT_STEP.md` / `.fi/pause.json` markers, or a human-review / clarify snapshot with no answer yet. The dashboard shows a **"Needs you"** badge (filterable) instead of "complete", the quest page keeps **Resume** and the right input control live (for older quests too, from the on-disk snapshots), and the engine clears those snapshots **both at the start of every run and on true completion** — so neither a finished quest *nor an actively-running resume* shows a stale pending state. (A refine/resume that continues past a pause used to leave the old `NEXT_STEP.md` / `pause.json` / human-review / clarify snapshot on disk for its whole run, mislabeling a busy quest as "Needs you"; the run-start clear drops those display markers while deliberately keeping the `*_answer.json` files, which are the input that resume is consuming.) **Add papers / data to any quest from the quest page** — a persistent upload writes into `inputs/papers` + `inputs/data` (the generalised `/api/quests/{id}/upload`), picked up on the next resume / refine — so you can feed a quest files from the frontend whenever, not only when a supply-pause fired. Quests with no `config.yaml` (CLI-started before configs were saved) disable Update/Resume with a clear note, since both need the original YAML.
- **Ensemble profile is an advanced quest decision** — `Multi-model ensemble` (`off` / `cross_check_only` / `ideate_and_check` / `full`) is under Advanced on `/interview`, the CLI's `--new` review screen (`a`) and VSCode's **Edit an advanced field**: it multiplies the cost, so it is off unless opened. The models are the user's to name: `Models for the ensemble` takes at least two ids (comma, semicolon or newline separated, duplicates dropped, order kept; the first is the moderator), and a profile with fewer than two named models configures no ensemble and says so in the YAML — the web form and `/api/interview/submit` refuse it with a 400 instead. VSCode offers the models `vscode.lm` reports as a multi-select. Every LLM `/tools/*` form (proposal / critique / digest / portfolio / summarize / analyze) carries the same picker and model field; the form passes the named models as a CSV via each tool's `--<tool>-ensemble` CLI flag. Default merge differs per tool: `tournament` for the canonical-synthesis tools (proposal / portfolio / summarize / analyze) and `synthesize` for the disagreement-preserving ones (critique / digest). Single-shot tools (digest / portfolio / summarize / critique / proposal) wrap one LLM call via `core.ensemble.run_singleshot_with_ensemble()`; `--analyze` runs a full engine quest and the ensemble lands on `cfg.provider.node_ensemble` for the `analyze` and `cross_check` nodes.
- **VSCode extension** — sanctioned `vscode.lm.*` integration with `@fi /new`, `/start`, `/fleet`, `/resume`, `/summarize`, `/proposal`, `/critique`, `/digest`, `/portfolio`, `/analyze`, `/update`, and `/probe [all]`, which asks the model selected in the Chat picker (or every model VS Code lists, after a confirmation that states the request count) whether text FI did not send appears to be in its context. It is behavioural evidence only: `vscode.lm` reports no usage figures, so the overhead itself is not measured, and the token counts it prints cover only what FI sent. **Which model a VS Code quest uses:** a quest started or resumed from the chat (`/start`, `/fleet`, `/resume`, `/new`) uses the model selected in the chat panel; it replaces `provider.model` from the config for that run (`launch.py --vscode-chat-model`, which only the extension passes; a step's own `provider.node_models` entry is kept), and `run.log` and the trace say so ("the chat panel's model X replaces Y from config.yaml"). With the chat picker on *Auto* no model is named and the config's stays. A run started from a terminal or the web page has no chat panel and uses `provider.model` from the config, as does `@fi /update`, which approves the config as it is and resumes with its model. On every run `run.log` names the model that answered the first call on the quest's main model, as the connection reports it (not when it names none, as with the picker on *Auto*), warns when it is not the one the config names, and warns — with a `model_changed` event in the trace — when it is not the model that answered the quest's earlier calls, since the results before and after were then made by different models. `.fi/cost.jsonl` names the model that answered each call.
- **Accessibility** — `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured on `marketing/index.html` (terminal fade-in + pulse-dot disabled when the OS preference is set).

### CLI tools

Everything from `--summarize` down to `--install-tectonic-from` below also has a friendlier spelling, `fi tools <name>` (e.g. `fi tools digest --days 7`) — the two are the exact same flag; `fi tools --help` lists them, and `fi --help-all` still shows the full flag form with every qualifier. `--config`, `--new`, `--serve`, `--resume`, `--update`, `--trace`, `--skills` and the skill-approval flags stay at the top level (`fi --help`), since most quests use one of those.

- **`--serve`** — local web UI; honours `FI_VSCODE_BRIDGE_PORT` env var for routing LLM calls through Copilot when launched from a VSCode terminal. Refuses `provider=vscode_extension` quests when no bridge is wired (400 at submit).
- **`--resume <quest_id>`** — re-enter the LangGraph from the last checkpointed node when a prior run died mid-pipeline.
- **Every quest on this computer, from any folder (`fi tools quests [--prune] [--json]`)** — each quest is recorded, when it starts, resumes or is renamed, in `~/.frontier-insight/quests.json` (`FI_HOME` moves the folder; `core/quest_index.py`): its folder, config, the folder it was started from, its title, when it started and was last seen. Several FI processes write it at once safely (a file lock, then a temporary file renamed over the old one; nothing is lost). Every place that takes a quest id — `--resume`, `--rerun`, `--watch`, `--from`, `--update`, `--critique`, `--trace`, `--why`, `fi tools rename`, the web quest page and its API, and VS Code's `@fi /resume`, `/plan`, `/watch`, `/rename`, `/why`, `/trace`, `/follow` and **FI: Quest map** — looks under the local outputs folder first, then in this list, then stops with the close matches. The id may be shortened to any unique start or end of it (at least 4 characters; lists show the 6 after the last dash). A quest found in another folder runs there: its own `config.yaml`, its own outputs folder, started from the folder it was started in (the CLI changes to that folder for a single-quest run; the web page and VS Code start it there); the web page will not move another folder's quest to its trash. An entry whose folder is gone is dropped when looked up or by `--prune` (one on a disconnected disk or share is only left out of lists); resuming a moved quest from its new folder records the new place. The web dashboard shows each quest's short id and lists **Quests in other folders** (`GET /api/quest-index`); VS Code offers them when the open folder has no quests.
- **`--fleet <yaml> ...`** — bounded concurrency, ref-counted proxies (per-provider locks so one provider's proxy warmup doesn't block others), `--memory-cap-mb` (a below-baseline cap is disabled with a warning, never deadlocked on), per-quest construction-failure isolation, and an optional process-wide LLM-call cap via `FI_MAX_CONCURRENT_LLM_CALLS`. `viztracer --profile` is single-quest only (rejected with `--fleet`).
- **`--summarize <folder>`** — walks mixed content, classifies each file, calls LLM once, writes `outputs/<summary_id>/summary.md`. Ingested into Axon.
- **`--digest --days N`** — weekly PM digest with WeekDiff (✅ promoted / 🆕 new / ⚠️ still-in-progress / 🛑 stalled / ❓ dropped) vs prior digest. Ingests as `fi_digest`.
- **`--portfolio`** — all-time cross-quest synthesis: topic clusters, near-duplicate detection, meta-paper candidates, coverage gaps. Ingests as `fi_portfolio`.
- **`--critique <id>`** — adversarial second-pass review (`--critique-provider <name>` to use a different model family). Writes `outputs/<id>/critique.md`. Ingests as `fi_critique`.
- **`--proposal "<topic>"`** — pre-quest planning doc + companion YAML under `outputs/_drafts/`. Companion YAML auto-pins the proposal into `knowledge.local_papers`. Ingests as `fi_proposal`.
- **`--analyze <data-path> --analyze-topic "..."`** — no-simulation quest on pre-staged data. Routes through `auto_collect_data → wait_for_data → data_load → analyze → write → review`. ~6 premium requests.
- **`--ingest <paths>`** — one-shot Axon ingest, no quest. Zero LLM calls.
- **`--rename <quest_id> <new title>`** (`fi tools rename`) — changes a finished or paused quest's title when the model's pick is a poor one (`core/quest_title.py`): the paper's title line (`paper/paper.md` and the copy at the quest root, or a front-matter `title:`), `config.yaml`'s `title` (a one-line edit, so its comments stay), `frontier_insight_summary.json`'s `title`, and the latest saved state, so resuming the quest (or `--rerun`) uses the new title. The change is a `title_changed` event in the audit trace, with the paper's hash before and after: on a finished quest it comes after the trace's seal, and `core/evidence.py` follows the paper's hash from the seal through these events, so a title change does not lower the evidence level (any other edit of the paper still does). Results, data and code are not touched. It is refused while the quest is running, and a title must be one line of at most 200 characters. A PDF, slides, poster or talk script already made keep the old title; the command names them and the `--resume <id> --emit <kind>` that makes each again (no re-run of the research). A rerun with `--from <step>` (the writing included) starts from a saved state from before the rename, so the writer may choose another title: rename again afterwards. A title that starts with `-` is given as `--title="..."`. The knowledge-layer copy written when the quest was accepted keeps the old title. The web quest page shows the title under the quest id with a **Rename** button (`POST /api/quests/{id}/title`), which offers to make the outputs again; in VS Code, `@fi /rename <quest_id> <new title>`. Zero LLM calls.
- **`--shadow-report`** (`fi tools shadow-report [--output-root DIR]`) — counts the recorded recommendations of past attempts (above) against what followed each decision, by action and by model. Counts only. Zero LLM calls.
- **`fi tools bench`** (for FI's developers; runs from a checkout of the repository) — FI's self-benchmark: tasks with a known answer (`dev/quest-topics/answers/`), errors planted on purpose in a copy of a recorded quest (a number of the paper changed, a number the run never computed added to the writer's answer, a factor taken out of the simulation, the analysis keeping one trial in five, a retracted paper among the search results), and a report of how often FI would still publish a wrong result, how often it holds back a right one, and which check caught each error first (`dev/evaluation/bench/README.md`). A planted copy's model calls are replayed from the recording up to the planted point and made for real after it, or all replayed with no model call at all; Crossref's retraction answers are recorded and replayed too. The answer is read from FI's own record of the trials, never from the paper. It has no web or VS Code page yet.
- **`--tidy-knowledge [check]`** (`fi tools tidy-knowledge`) — finds quest results the knowledge layer keeps under both standings (accepted and preliminary; left by write-backs from before a standing change removed the other copy) and removes the older copy, so an out-of-date accepted result can no longer be cited, and takes quests that are now preliminary only off the entries of the papers they cited; `check` only lists them. Zero LLM calls.
- **`--install-tectonic`** — drops a self-bootstrapping LaTeX binary (~70 MB) into `tools/`. SHA-256 verified.
- **`--install-tectonic-from <path>`** — airgapped variant: install from a locally-staged archive or extracted binary. No network call. ELF / Mach-O / PE header sanity check.
- **Resumable quests** — SQLite-checkpointed state at `<quest_root>/.fi/state.sqlite`.

## Provider matrix

| Provider | Transport | Auth | ToS standing |
|---|---|---|---|
| `openai` | HTTP direct | `OPENAI_API_KEY` env | ✅ Sanctioned |
| `codex` | HTTP direct | `OPENAI_API_KEY` env | ✅ Sanctioned |
| `gemini` | HTTP direct | `GEMINI_API_KEY` env | ✅ Sanctioned |
| `ollama`, `vllm` | HTTP direct (local) | none | ✅ Self-hosted |
| `claude_cli` | CLI exec | `claude auth login` (Claude Pro/Max OAuth) | ✅ Sanctioned |
| `codex_cli` | CLI exec | `codex login` (ChatGPT Plus/Pro OAuth) | ✅ Sanctioned |
| `copilot_cli` | CLI exec | `gh auth login` (Copilot subscription) | ⚠️ Agentic — replies conversationally; use `vscode_extension` for Copilot instead. |
| `gemini_cli` | CLI exec | `gemini` OAuth / Google AI Studio key | ⚠️ Google has been migrating individual-account sign-in to Antigravity — verified live (`Code Assist for individuals... migrate to the Antigravity suite`, rc=1 before reaching FI). Try `antigravity_cli` or the direct `gemini` (API-key) provider instead. |
| **`vscode_extension`** | **VSCode bridge** | **whatever the model is signed in as — Copilot Chat, or another extension's provider** | **✅ Sanctioned via `vscode.lm`** |
| `claude_code` | HTTP via proxy | `claude auth login` + spawned wrapper | ⚠️ Third-party wrapper |
| `github_copilot_cli` | HTTP via proxy | `gh auth login` + spawned `copilot-api` | ⚠️ Against ToS spirit (use `copilot_cli` instead) |
| `github_copilot_vscode` | HTTP via proxy | VSCode Copilot extension + spawned `copilot-api` | ⚠️ Against ToS spirit (use `vscode_extension` instead) |

`github_copilot_*` providers AND `copilot_cli` emit a one-time warning
at engine init. Set `FI_SUPPRESS_PROXY_WARN=1` to silence (use at your
own risk).

**CLI calls are answer-only.** `codex_cli` and `claude_cli` run with web search, shell and code execution, MCP servers, skills, plugins, subagents and memory turned off, and do not save a session. Every CLI call, for every CLI provider, starts in a new, empty temporary directory that is removed when the call ends (also after an error or timeout), so a CLI never sees FI's working directory. `codex_cli` does not read `~/.codex/config.toml`: custom model providers, profiles and MCP servers defined there are not used, the model comes from `provider.model` (blank = codex's own default, not config.toml's) and the effort from `provider.reasoning_effort`. Not restricted yet: `antigravity_cli` (no option turns its tools off, and it works in its own fixed workspace), `copilot_cli` (keeps `--allow-all-tools`; not checkable while the Copilot quota was used up) and `gemini_cli` (keeps `--yolo`; the Gemini CLI no longer signs in individual Google accounts). Flags and measurements: [PROVIDERS.md](PROVIDERS.md#answer-only-cli-calls).

## Knowledge layer — three-layer retrieval

```
asearch(query)
  ├─ 1. Pinned local_papers (always first)
  ├─ 2. Axon (your long-term store; falls through on empty)
  └─ 3. External router: agent picks 1–5 sources from the catalog
        ├─ openalex / arxiv / crossref / semantic_scholar / pubmed / core / google_scholar
        └─ (optional) full text of free copies only
```

After an **accepted** quest, the write-back lays down a structured
bundle so Axon stays title-searchable and topic-linkable:

| kind | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `fi_paper_spine` | One tight chunk per paper — title / authors / DOI / keywords / abstract / key claims. Title queries hit this directly, and the keywords the writer picked (shown under a scientific paper's abstract, kept out of sight in a report, brief, essay or whitepaper) give later searches the paper's subject terms. |
| `fi_quest_paper` | Full paper body with a 1-line `[Title · Year · Venue · DOI]` citation header on every chunk. |
| `fi_quest_summary` | Structured-findings JSON (hypothesis, key_findings, result_json, verdict, score, model). |
| `fi_topic_event` | One pointer per accepted quest keyed by topic slug — enables "what do we know about X" rollups. |
| `fi_external_ref_spine` × N | Curated card-catalog entries for cited papers. Only refs from *accepted* quests persist. |

A written-back result is **accepted evidence** only when the quest was a study (`result_use: research` or `decision`, or
`rigor_profile: research`), a reviewer really accepted it, a person accepted it at the review pause, and its evidence level
is `publication_ready` (an automatic accept stops one level below it). Anything else
that is written back — an exploration, a study with evidence gaps, or (with `knowledge.write_back_only_on_accept: false`)
a result nobody accepted — is kept as **preliminary**: under `fi_preliminary_spine` / `fi_preliminary_paper` /
`fi_preliminary_summary` instead of the three kinds above, its topic event starts with `PRELIMINARY`, no ref spines are
written for it, and every record carries `standing`, `result_use`, `evidence_status` and `review_status`. A later quest
never cites a preliminary result or lists it among its sources: it sees it in a separate short block, "Earlier FI results
kept as PRELIMINARY … not evidence", as a reminder of what was tried. It is also never written into a quest's data (`data/literature/`, `data/auto_collected/`) or counted among its
sources, never written back as another quest's external reference, and the web page's re-ingest keeps every paper
it re-ingests as preliminary (it has no review to trust). Records written before this existed carry no `standing`
and are read as before. A quest re-run whose standing changes removes its copy under the other kinds before writing the new one (every chunk of it; first, because Axon skips a text it has stored before), so an accepted copy never outlives a preliminary result. When that copy cannot be removed (the Axon service is unreachable, say), or the knowledge base cannot show there is none, nothing new is written: the write-back says it failed, and `run.log` and the quest's card (`.fi/knowledge_problem.json`) say to run `fi tools tidy-knowledge` once the service is reachable and then resume the quest (tidy lists which quests to resume); a write that fails after the quest's earlier copy was removed says so the same way. The note is settled again at every write-back, and a finished quest that skips its write-back keeps it only while the knowledge base still holds both copies. After a write, the knowledge base is checked again for a leftover copy. A cited paper's entry in the knowledge base (`fi_external_ref_spine`) names every accepted quest that used the paper (`consumed_by_quests`, also as its last line, *CITED BY FI QUESTS*), not only the last one; a quest whose result becomes preliminary is taken off its papers' entries, and an entry no accepted quest uses any more is removed. Entries are read, removed and written again under one lock that every FI process shares, and an entry removed is written back as it was if the new write fails; an entry that cannot be read is left as it is. The paper's own source (arXiv, OpenAlex, ...) is kept as `ref_source`. `fi tools tidy-knowledge` removes the older copy that earlier write-backs left behind, takes quests that are now preliminary only off old entries (only when the knowledge base is shown to hold no accepted copy of them), and writes again the entries an older write-back stored in a way that hid them from the service's list. Over the Axon service, a text removed and written again can still be skipped, because the service's removal leaves its per-piece duplicate records behind (an open Axon issue): FI checks which documents landed and says in `run.log` when one did not. When `tidy-knowledge` cannot tell which copy is newer, it removes nothing and says so.

## Configuration

See [USAGE.md](USAGE.md) for the full annotated YAML schema. Quick
landmarks:

- `provider.*` — provider name, model, reasoning effort, per-node model routing, ensemble.
- `engine.*` — max_iterations, review_panel, ensemble, no_simulation, survey_mode (literature/history synthesis: no experiment and no dataset), and the rest of the research-loop knobs.
- `pauses.*` — the unified human-in-the-loop section: clarify, papers, supply, plan, review, auto_accept_on_pass, timeout_s (legacy `engine.*` / `knowledge.*` pause flags still map in).
- `execution.*` — venv vs docker, timeout, python version, the Docker sandbox's memory / CPU / process limits.
- `knowledge.*` — Axon config, top_k (RAG) + external_top_k (web), relevance_min_score (literature relevance floor) + relevance_min_keep, local_papers, full-text fetch.
- `output.*` — kinds, paper_format, audience, output_dir, require_pdf.

## Example quests

| Example | Provider used | Topic |
|---|---|---|
| [`integrator_bakeoff`](../examples/integrator_bakeoff/config.yaml) | (any) | Three numerical integrators on a damped harmonic oscillator (RK4 / Velocity-Verlet / forward Euler). |
| [`bootstrap_ci_coverage`](../examples/bootstrap_ci_coverage/config.yaml) | `ollama` → cloud-routed reasoning models | How often bootstrap and t confidence intervals contain the true mean for small, skewed samples (n = 5 to 100). |
| [`bernstein_vazirani_noise`](../examples/bernstein_vazirani_noise/config.yaml) | `claude_cli` (reuses `claude auth login` OAuth) | Bernstein-Vazirani algorithm under per-gate depolarizing noise. |

## Tests

Pytest-based, Windows-native Python 3.11+, fake LLMs everywhere
(`monkeypatch.setattr` on `LLMClient.chat`) — zero real API spend in
CI. A few tests skip gracefully when their external tool isn't on
PATH (Docker daemon, Marp CLI, pdflatex).

## Architecture

See [`architecture.md`](architecture.md) for the layered diagram and
contracts (`Config`, `QuestState`, `QuestArtifacts`, `Executor`,
generator protocol).
